# Paint Template Nodes

`paint_template_nodes/mod.rs` is the retained-host template-node paint entry. It should stay as a structural module declaration surface plus stable re-exports for Workbench painters and test helpers.

`template_node_pipeline.rs` owns the public draw pipeline entry. Its `draw.rs` child iterates node models, applies clipping, orders runtime commands, and reports whether any visible node was painted. Its `clip.rs` child owns node clip resolution, and `test_support.rs` owns image-buffer helpers used by the template paint regression suite.

`template_nodes.rs` owns command emission for a single template node. The `template_nodes/` child owners keep command production separated into command orchestration, fallback rendering, frame geometry, ordering, and specialized component dispatch. Rendering DTO conversion remains in `render_command_conversion.rs`, while `render_commands.rs` owns the runtime command paint harness used by tests.

The 2026-07-04 retained popup row preference-route pass moves popup/picker row text and adornment geometry off component-local constants. `template_popup_rows/metrics.rs` owns `WorkbenchPopupRowMetrics` projected from `HostControlMetrics`; `template_popup_rows/text/{label,shortcut}.rs`, `template_popup_rows/surface/row.rs`, and `template_popup_row_adornments/geometry.rs` consume font size, line height, text inset, minimum text slot height, surface radius, outline width, and trailing adornment size/reserve from that projection. Popup row text still emits default UI text style rather than a concrete family, so font family, color theme, and density/style choices remain globally switchable through retained-host preferences. The same pass fixed a test-only relative import in `render_command_conversion/style/text.rs` so current retained text tests can compile without changing production text conversion behavior.

The 2026-07-06 popup-row text/shortcut metrics follow-up tightens that owner boundary. `WorkbenchPopupRowMetrics` now also owns the minimum text width and shortcut column fractions, while `template_popup_rows/text.rs` owns the semantic popup-row `UiTextRunPaintStyle` helper. `text/label.rs`, `text/shortcut.rs`, and `surface/separator.rs` now consume those projected values instead of spelling local `1.0`, `0.58`, `0.38`, `8.0`, or `16.0` geometry values. Shortcut, label, and separator geometry therefore move together when the Workbench density and text metrics change.

Validation for the popup-row text/shortcut metrics follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a static scan confirming shortcut ratios and default text style only remain in their owners, projection assertions for `min_text_rect_width` and shortcut fractions, and a repo-target popup/editor/workbench screenshot scan returning no matches. Focused Cargo and screenshot refresh remain deferred while other cargo/rustc lanes are active, so this entry is not final popup-row, dropdown popup, or window-level text evidence.

The 2026-07-04 retained status icon glyph preference-route pass moves the status icon button glyph display size off a public component-local constant. `template_status_control_geometry/metrics.rs` now projects `WorkbenchStatusMetrics.icon_glyph_size` from `HostControlMetrics`, and `template_status_control_geometry/icons.rs` consumes that value when centering the glyph in the icon button frame. `template_status_glyphs/geometry.rs` keeps 16x16 only as a private canonical vector canvas; `local_rect(...)` and `centered_canvas_rect(...)` scale authored segments to the actual glyph frame. This preserves global density/theme/font preference routing while preventing status glyph geometry from freezing the final UI size at 16 px.

The 2026-07-04 retained field/search/stepper preference-route pass moves text and search fields off local production metrics while keeping font selection global. `template_fields/metrics.rs` owns `WorkbenchFieldMetrics` projected from `HostControlMetrics`; `template_fields/surface.rs`, `template_fields/search.rs`, `template_fields/text.rs`, and `template_field_stepper::{metrics,command}.rs` consume border, radius, font size, line height, input padding, search icon/text inset, max field height, fallback ring, stepper width, divider placement, and glyph geometry from that projection. `style_selector/workbench_text_field` owns the `stepper_divider` palette role projected from `HostMaterialPalette`. Field text still emits default UI text style rather than a concrete family, so font family, theme color, and control style remain globally switchable through retained-host preferences.

The 2026-07-06 field stepper fallback glyph typed-grid follow-up separates glyph shape ownership from the field metrics owner. `template_field_stepper/metrics.rs` now only exposes the projected stepper size, divider, and glyph slot metrics. `template_field_stepper/segments.rs` owns `FieldStepperGlyphSegmentSpec`, the 50x80 subpixel grid that preserves the old 10x16 stepper canvas and 1.4 px strokes, and the deterministic fallback segment list. `command.rs` consumes `push_stepper_glyph_segments(...)` after arranging the glyph rect from `workbench_field_stepper_metrics()`, so layout metrics and fallback vector geometry stay separate.

Validation for the field stepper fallback follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a static scan returning `NO_FIELD_STEPPER_RAW_GLYPH_TUPLES_OR_INLINE_SCALE_LITERALS`, line-count checks, and a target editor/field/stepper/glyph screenshot scan returning `NO_TARGET_EDITOR_FIELD_STEPPER_GLYPH_SCREENSHOT_MATCHES`. Focused Cargo and screenshot refresh remain deferred while other cargo/rustc lanes are active, so this entry is not final TextField, SearchField, input-group, or window-level visual evidence.

The 2026-06-28 logical text-align support gate keeps runtime render-command text positioning inside `render_command_conversion/style/text.rs`. Projection code may preserve `UiTextAlign::Start` and `UiTextAlign::End` as semantic strings for metadata, but the paint conversion leaf resolves those logical values against `UiTextDirection` before computing x placement. `Auto`, `LeftToRight`, and `Mixed` use the LTR fallback; `RightToLeft` flips Start and End. The focused regression locks this in the retained paint owner so future runtime-interface enum additions do not reopen non-exhaustive matches or push alignment policy into Asset Browser or view assembly roots.

The 2026-07-04 render-command alignment resolved-family follow-up keeps that same owner responsible for the width used by Center/Right placement. `render_command_conversion/style/text.rs::aligned_text_x(...)` now builds a retained runtime measure style from the command `UiResolvedStyle` before calling `measure_text_size(...)`, using `text_paint_style_from_resolved_style(...)`, `font_face_for_paint_style(...)`, and `runtime_text_style_for_face(...)` to match the retained glyph-paint font family. This closes the case where command metadata still carried a generic/requested family while the final glyphs were painted with DengXian/等线. It does not add a component-local font family, ZUI asset shortcut, or root painter branch.

The 2026-07-06 template text alignment metric follow-up splits Center/Right placement arithmetic out of `render_command_conversion/style/text.rs`. `render_command_conversion/style/text/metrics.rs` now owns measured-width clamping, remaining-space clamping, and the center alignment factor, while `text.rs` keeps logical alignment resolution and runtime measure-style construction. This keeps text conversion aligned with the runtime text plan without reintroducing heuristic character-count measurement or component-local font choices.

The 2026-07-04 divider label bounds runtime-measure follow-up keeps Material Divider geometry inside the material primitive owner while removing a text-width heuristic. `material_primitives/divider/geometry/label_bounds/horizontal.rs` now measures label gaps with the retained text measurement helper and the same rect-height-derived font size used by `horizontal_label_text_frame(...)`; `material_primitives/divider/horizontal.rs` only passes the existing rect down to that owner. The root template painter, ZUI assets, component-local fonts, and render-command text conversion path stay unchanged.

The 2026-07-06 Material Divider text metrics owner follow-up keeps that divider text geometry on a local metrics route instead of leaving line-height, wrapper width, vertical centering, vertical text padding, and minimum text-frame extents in leaf modules. `material_primitives/divider/geometry/metrics.rs` now owns `divider_label_line_height(...)`, `divider_wrapped_label_width(...)`, `divider_centered_label_y(...)`, `divider_vertical_label_height(...)`, `divider_vertical_text_horizontal_padding(...)`, and `divider_min_text_frame_extent(...)`; `text_frame.rs`, `label_bounds/horizontal.rs`, and `label_bounds/vertical.rs` consume those helpers. `label_bounds/align.rs` owns the Left/Center/Right placement ratios so horizontal bounds no longer embeds the raw alignment factors. Runtime text measurement remains the width source, and no component-local font family, root painter branch, or ZUI shortcut was added.

The 2026-07-04 chip label runtime-measure follow-up keeps Material Chip text geometry inside the chip primitive owner while removing its matching text-width heuristic. `material_primitives/chip/geometry/label.rs` now measures the label frame with `measure_runtime_text_width(label, chip_font_size(node))`, and `material_primitives/chip/geometry/metrics.rs` no longer exposes `CHIP_LABEL_WIDTH_RATIO`. Existing chip padding, avatar/icon/delete slots, clamp behavior, root template painter, ZUI assets, and component-local font policy stay unchanged.

The 2026-07-06 Material Chip label text metrics owner follow-up keeps that same primitive text geometry on the chip metrics route. `material_primitives/chip/geometry/metrics.rs` now owns `chip_font_size(...)`, `chip_label_base_padding(...)`, `chip_label_line_height(...)`, `chip_label_width(...)`, and `chip_label_y(...)`, so `geometry/label.rs` no longer embeds the 1.5 line-height ratio, 0.5 vertical center ratio, 1 px minimum label width, or font-size/padding fallback selection. The label leaf still owns composition with avatar/icon/delete slots, and runtime text measurement remains the width source.

The 2026-07-04 remaining material primitive runtime-measure follow-up clears the remaining Material primitive text-width ratios without moving ownership. Alert message frames, Avatar text frames, Badge root text frames, and Badge overlay frame/text sizing now call the retained text measurement helper, while their metrics modules no longer expose `ALERT_TEXT_WIDTH_RATIO`, `AVATAR_TEXT_WIDTH_RATIO`, `BADGE_ROOT_TEXT_WIDTH_RATIO`, or `BADGE_TEXT_WIDTH_RATIO`. Existing action/icon padding, center alignment, badge min-width/padding, overlay anchor behavior, root template painter, ZUI assets, and component-local font policy stay unchanged.

The 2026-07-06 Material Alert message text metrics owner follow-up keeps the alert text frame on the alert metrics route. `material_primitives/alert/geometry/metrics.rs` now owns `alert_font_size(...)`, `alert_message_line_height(...)`, `alert_message_width(...)`, and `alert_message_y(...)`, so `geometry/message.rs` no longer embeds the 1.45 line-height ratio, 1 px minimum message width, 0.5 vertical center ratio, or font-size fallback. Message left/right still composes the existing icon and action slots, and retained runtime text measurement remains the width source.

The 2026-07-06 Material Avatar text metrics owner follow-up keeps avatar fallback text geometry on `material_primitives/avatar/geometry/metrics.rs`. That owner now exposes `avatar_font_size(...)`, `avatar_text_line_height(...)`, `avatar_text_width(...)`, `avatar_centered_text_x(...)`, and `avatar_centered_text_y(...)`; `geometry/text.rs` consumes those helpers after measuring the label through retained runtime text. The text leaf no longer embeds the font ratio fallback, minimum font-size clamp, 1 px minimum width, or 0.5 center placement policy.

The 2026-07-06 Material Badge text metrics owner follow-up keeps both root badge text and overlay badge text on `material_primitives/badge/geometry/metrics.rs`. The owner now holds root font fallback, text line-height, root inset available width, shared text width clamp, root x placement, centered text x/y, overlay font size, overlay text line-height, overlay size from measured text plus padding, and overlay rect projection. `root_text.rs` and `overlay.rs` continue to request retained runtime text measurement, while anchor and radius decisions stay in their existing owners.

The 2026-07-04 dialog action runtime-measure follow-up applies the same width discipline to the non-Material dialog action owner. `template_dialogs/actions/labels.rs::action_width(...)` now uses the retained runtime text measurement helper with `dialog_metrics().action_font_size`, `dialog_metrics().action_text_padding_x`, and `dialog_metrics().action_text_clip_guard`; the later S15.4eq pass absorbed the former action metrics child into `template_dialogs/metrics.rs` and removed the old child file. `actions/commands.rs` remains the owner for confirm/cancel ordering, right alignment, gaps, and fallback labels, while dialog geometry and action sizing now share the same host metrics projection.

`style_selector/mod.rs` is now the structural Workbench style-selector entry. `style_selector/exports.rs` owns the selector re-export surface for the child style modules while each `workbench_*` child keeps the family-specific style resolution.

The 2026-06-27 popup/dropdown row styling pass originally kept menu row color policy in `style_selector/workbench_popup_row/selection.rs` and popup shell geometry in `template_popup_rows/surface/*`. That pass moved popup rows off bright cyan fills and onto low-emphasis row feedback, but still expressed selected and checked state through a left selection marker.

The 2026-06-27 selected-only list row follow-up keeps row identity and checked state separate. `template_list_row_glyphs/selection.rs` maps only `checked` rows to the trailing `Check` adornment, while selected-only rows keep the navigation chevron. `style_selector/workbench_list_row/selection.rs` still treats selected and checked rows as marked for row surface/text, but only checked rows mark the trailing adornment color. This keeps selected list rows on a low-emphasis surface with a left 2 px indicator instead of adding a bright right-side checked marker.

The 2026-06-29 Workbench row selected-outline pass supersedes that left-marker-only selected row language for regular table, list, and tree rows. `style_selector/workbench_table_row`, `style_selector/workbench_list_row/surface.rs`, and `style_selector/workbench_tree_row/surface.rs` now resolve selected/checked rows to `PALETTE.surface_selected` plus a 1 px `PALETTE.accent` outline, while unmarked focused or pressed rows keep the keyboard `PALETTE.focus_ring` border. `template_table_rows/surface.rs`, `template_list_rows/surface.rs`, and `template_tree_rows/surface.rs` no longer paint a separate left selection indicator; selected table rows also skip the bottom separator so the rounded accent outline remains visible. Focused style and paint regressions cover all three row families, and the component atlas plus M3 screenshots were refreshed under `docs/tests/editor`.

The later 2026-06-29 popup/dropdown row selected-outline pass applies the same full-row rule to popup, dropdown, picker, and command-palette rows. `style_selector/workbench_popup_row/model.rs` now exposes `outline` instead of `selection_mark`, and `selection.rs` resolves selected or checked rows to `PALETTE.surface_selected` plus a 1 px `PALETTE.accent` outline while leaving keyboard focus separate. `template_popup_rows/surface/row.rs` paints that outline on the row quad instead of a left marker. `template_command_palette/rows/surface.rs` consumes the same style, and the old command-palette `selection_mark_rect` geometry constants were removed so the marker path cannot return through the search-popup variant. Pixel regressions in `template_popup_rows_tests/paint.rs` lock the selected fill, the accent outline, and absence of the old left strip; the component atlas and M3 screenshots were refreshed under `docs/tests/editor`.

The 2026-06-30 row/popup selected-fill de-emphasis pass supersedes the 2026-06-29 selected fill tone while keeping the full-row outline contract. Regular table, list, tree, popup, dropdown, picker, and command-palette selected or checked rows now use `PALETTE.surface_pressed` for the row fill and reserve `PALETTE.surface_selected` for higher-emphasis controls that still need it. `style_selector/workbench_table_row/palette.rs`, `style_selector/workbench_list_row/surface.rs`, `style_selector/workbench_tree_row/surface.rs`, and `style_selector/workbench_popup_row/{palette,selection}.rs` own that state-to-fill mapping; the paint-template row owners still only consume the resolved style. Pixel regressions lock the dark fill and absence of `PALETTE.surface_selected` inside selected row interiors, and the component atlas was refreshed under `docs/tests/editor`.

The later 2026-06-30 row/popup selected-outline de-emphasis pass moves the static selected outline into `style_selector/workbench_row_selection.rs`. Table, list, tree, popup, dropdown, picker, and command-palette selected or checked rows now draw a 1 px `PALETTE.border` outline instead of `PALETTE.accent`; `PALETTE.focus_ring` remains reserved for focused, pressed, dragging, and drop-hover states. This keeps the selected state visible in local row components while preventing complex lists and popups from carrying a full cyan rectangle in composed windows. Style and paint regressions assert the neutral outline and the absence of accent pixels on selected row borders. Validation used scoped rustfmt, focused style/paint direct tests 16/16, the full editor-host build, and the ignored component-atlas screenshot harness; the refreshed PNG is `docs/tests/editor/editor-components-workbench-slate-atlas-900x620.png`, and repo `target` has no matching atlas screenshot.

The 2026-07-06 shared row-selection palette-owner follow-up keeps that neutral selected outline behind `WorkbenchRowSelectionPalette`. `selected_row_outline_color()` now consumes `workbench_row_selection_palette()` projected from `HostMaterialPalette.border`, and `selected_row_outline_color_from_palette(...)` gives private regressions a pure route. List, table, tree, popup, dropdown, picker, and command-palette rows keep the same neutral border semantics while the shared leaf no longer directly reads the global palette.

The 2026-07-06 Workbench list-row palette-owner follow-up keeps list row surface, text, and trailing adornment tones behind `WorkbenchListRowPalette`. `style_selector/workbench_list_row/palette.rs` projects marked/pressed surface, hot surface, focus border, text, muted text, disabled text, and marked adornment roles from `HostMaterialPalette`; `colors.rs` and `surface.rs` consume `workbench_list_row_palette()` instead of reading `current_host_palette()` directly. Authored `value_color` and `icon_color` overrides still win, disabled/loading rows still suppress row chrome and use disabled text, selected/checked rows still use the low-emphasis pressed surface plus the shared neutral row-selection outline, and unmarked focused/pressed/drag/drop rows still use the focus border.

The 2026-07-06 Workbench tree-row palette-owner follow-up applies the same owner route to tree row surface, primary/icon/secondary/action text, and action color decisions. `style_selector/workbench_tree_row/palette.rs` now owns `WorkbenchTreeRowPalette`, projected from `HostMaterialPalette`; `colors.rs` and `surface.rs` consume `workbench_tree_row_palette()` instead of reading `current_host_palette()` directly. The focused-only hover split remains intact: an unmarked focused tree row has no hover fill and keeps the keyboard focus border, while an unmarked hovered row keeps only the hover fill. Selected and checked tree rows still use the low-emphasis pressed surface plus the shared neutral row-selection outline, and disabled/loading rows still suppress row chrome and use disabled text.

The 2026-07-06 Workbench table-row palette-owner follow-up completes the same route for table row border decisions. `style_selector/workbench_table_row/palette.rs` owns `WorkbenchTableRowPalette`, projected from `HostMaterialPalette`, and `colors/{background,action,border}.rs` plus row text selection consume `workbench_table_row_palette()` instead of letting border logic read `current_host_palette()` directly. Marked rows keep the low-emphasis pressed fill and shared neutral row-selection outline, drag/drop marked rows still use the keyboard focus ring, unavailable rows still suppress borders and use disabled tones, and header/tail rows stay on the recessed table surface.

The 2026-07-06 popup row selection palette follow-up keeps the selection leaf on the popup-row owner route. `style_selector/workbench_popup_row/palette.rs` now owns `WorkbenchPopupRowPalette`, projected by `workbench_popup_row_palette_from_host(...)`, with marked/hot background plus normal, muted, disabled, and danger text roles. `style_selector/workbench_popup_row/selection.rs` consumes that owner instead of reading `current_host_palette()` directly; selected/focused outline remains delegated to the shared `workbench_row_selection::selected_row_outline_color()` owner so popup rows stay aligned with list/table/tree row selection outlines.

Validation for the popup row selection palette follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a selection-leaf scan returning `NO_POPUP_ROW_SELECTION_DIRECT_HOST_PALETTE_MATCHES`, and a target/external target editor/workbench screenshot scan returning clean. Focused Cargo and screenshot refresh remain deferred while an unrelated runtime Cargo lane is active, so this entry is not final popup, dropdown, command-palette row, or window-level visual evidence.

The 2026-06-30 segmented selected-fill de-emphasis pass applies the same low-emphasis rule to tab/segmented controls. `style_selector/workbench_segmented_control/palette.rs` defines `WORKBENCH_SEGMENT_SELECTED_BACKGROUND` as `PALETTE.surface_pressed`, and `segments.rs` maps available selected segments to that background while unavailable segments still use the disabled surface. `template_segmented_controls/segments/selected.rs` remains a paint consumer: it draws the resolved selected surface and the existing underline geometry, with the 2 px `PALETTE.accent` underline carrying the selection identity. Style and pixel regressions lock the dark fill, accent underline, and absence of `PALETTE.surface_selected` in the selected segment interior.

The 2026-07-03 segmented/tab metrics preference pass keeps this component family on the global control-metrics route. `template_segmented_control_geometry/metrics.rs` owns `WorkbenchSegmentedControlMetrics` and the pure projection from `HostControlMetrics`; segment/tab font size, line height, text inset, group-label height/gap, selected inset, divider width/inset, and tab underline height now flow from `current_host_metrics()`. The segmented divider helper consumes projected width/inset values instead of hardcoding local geometry. No concrete font family or component-local code/mono style route was added, so typography and density remain globally switchable through appearance preferences.

The 2026-07-06 segmented selector palette/metrics follow-up keeps the style selector leaves on a matching owner route. `style_selector/workbench_segmented_control/palette.rs` now owns `WorkbenchSegmentedControlPalette`, projected by `workbench_segmented_control_palette_from_host(...)`, for idle/hot/pressed/disabled backgrounds, normal/active/focus/disabled borders, selected segment surface/border/underline, selected/idle/disabled text, and group-label text. `style_selector/workbench_segmented_control/metrics.rs` owns the selector border width and selected-underline fallback height projected from `HostControlMetrics`. `control.rs`, `segments.rs`, and `text.rs` now consume those owners instead of reading `PALETTE`, `METRICS`, or production `WORKBENCH_SEGMENT_*` constants directly; the remaining constants are test-only fixture aliases.

Validation for the segmented selector palette/metrics follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a leaf scan returning `NO_SEGMENTED_CONTROL_LEAF_STATIC_PALETTE_OR_METRICS_MATCHES`, and a target/external target editor/workbench screenshot scan returning clean. Focused Cargo and screenshot refresh remain deferred while the active runtime Cargo lane is unrelated to this slice, so this entry is not final segmented control, drawer tab, button-group, or window-level visual evidence.

The 2026-07-04 template-chip preference-route pass applies the same rule to retained viewport chips/tags. `template_chips/metrics.rs` owns `WorkbenchChipMetrics` and projects chip font size, line height, radius, border width, and label insets from `HostControlMetrics`; `template_chips/style.rs` owns `WorkbenchChipPalette` and projects normal, hover, pressed, disabled, focus, text, muted text, disabled text, and border roles from `HostMaterialPalette`. `geometry.rs`, `surface.rs`, and `text.rs` only consume those projections. Chip labels still emit `HostPaintCommand::text` with `UiTextRunPaintStyle::default()`, so actual font selection stays in the global retained-host text preference/runtime text chain instead of being pinned inside the chip painter.

The 2026-07-06 chip chevron follow-up keeps the dropdown affordance on that same chip metrics route. `template_chips/metrics.rs` now projects chevron size, right inset, and text reserve from `current_host_metrics()`, preserving the current 12 px chevron, 8 px right inset, and 24 px reserve defaults while letting density and typography move the chip as one unit. `template_chip_glyphs/metrics.rs` forwards those values, and both `template_chip_glyphs/chevron.rs` and `template_chips/text.rs` consume them so glyph placement and label clipping share one owner. The 12 px segment grid remains only the fallback vector-glyph canvas, not a layout metric.

Validation for the chip chevron follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a chip-owner scan returning `NO_CHIP_CHEVRON_LOCAL_METRIC_CONSTANTS`, and a target/external target editor screenshot scan returning clean. Focused Cargo and screenshot refresh remain deferred while other cargo/rustc lanes are active, so this entry is not final chip/tag/toolbar-filter screenshot evidence.

The 2026-07-06 chip chevron fallback glyph typed-grid follow-up keeps that vector fallback responsive without leaving raw segment tuples in the chevron leaf. `template_chip_glyphs/segments.rs` now owns `ChipGlyphSegmentSpec` plus the 12-unit chevron grid projection, while `template_chip_glyphs/chevron.rs` declares integer grid segments. The chip-level metrics owner still decides chevron size, right inset, and text reserve; this follow-up only standardizes the deterministic fallback geometry.

Validation for the chip chevron fallback follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a static scan returning `NO_CHIP_FALLBACK_GLYPH_RAW_PIXEL_TUPLES`, line-count checks, and a target editor/chip/glyph screenshot scan returning `NO_TARGET_EDITOR_CHIP_GLYPH_SCREENSHOT_MATCHES`. Focused Cargo and screenshot refresh remain deferred while other cargo/rustc lanes are active, so this entry is not final chip/tag/toolbar-filter or window-level visual evidence.

The 2026-07-06 chip layer-order owner follow-up moves retained Workbench chip label and chevron paint order into `template_chips/layers.rs`. The command leaf now consumes `label_order(...)` and `chevron_order(...)` instead of writing direct `order + 2` and `order + 3`, keeping chip surface, text, and dropdown affordance stacking local to the chip family before toolbar filters or viewport mode selectors compose it.

Validation for the chip layer-order owner follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a static scan returning `NO_TEMPLATE_CHIPS_DIRECT_ORDER_OFFSETS`, and a repo `target` chip/layer/editor/workbench screenshot scan with no matches. Focused Cargo and screenshot refresh remain deferred while other cargo/rustc lanes are active, so this entry is not final chip/tag/toolbar-filter or window-level visual evidence.

The 2026-07-06 selection-control layer-order owner follow-up moves checkbox/radio/toggle internal paint order into `template_selection_controls/layers.rs`. Checkbox and radio now consume `mark_content_order(...)` for tick/dot content and `mark_label_order(...)` for labels, while toggle consumes `toggle_label_order(...)` and `toggle_thumb_order(...)`; the leaf painters keep only surface, mark, label, and thumb command orchestration. This keeps the UE/Slate-style 16 px mark brush stack local to the selection-control family before multi-select rows, popup filters, or toolbar groups compose it.

Validation for the selection-control layer-order owner follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a static scan returning `NO_TEMPLATE_SELECTION_CONTROLS_DIRECT_ORDER_OFFSETS`, and a repo `target` selection/checkbox/radio/toggle/layer/editor/workbench screenshot scan with no matches. Focused Cargo and `docs/tests/editor` screenshot refresh remain deferred while other cargo/rustc lanes are active, so this entry is not final selection-control, multi-select, or window-level visual evidence.

The 2026-07-06 slider layer-order owner follow-up moves retained Workbench Slider internal paint order into `template_sliders/layers.rs`. Track, sequence, thumb, and text leaves consume named helpers for track fill, ticks, label, range-min thumb, primary thumb, value surface/text, and thumb body instead of spelling raw `order + N` offsets. This keeps slider and range-slider stacking local to the slider family before property rows, tool settings, or composed drawers reuse it.

Validation for the slider layer-order owner follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a static scan returning `NO_TEMPLATE_SLIDERS_DIRECT_ORDER_OFFSETS`, and a repo `target` slider/range/value/editor/workbench screenshot scan with no matches. Focused Cargo and `docs/tests/editor` screenshot refresh remain deferred while other cargo/rustc lanes are active, so this entry is not final slider, range-control, property-row, or window-level visual evidence.

The 2026-06-29 checkbox tick asset pass keeps selection-control checked indicator drawing in `template_selection_controls/checkbox/tick.rs` instead of spreading special cases through the style selectors. Checked checkboxes first render the semantic `checkmark` shell SVG through `template_icon_assets::push_icon_asset_pixels(...)` at the existing 16 px mark rect, tinted with `PALETTE.accent`, and retain the former three-quad tick only as an asset-missing fallback. The local regression `checked_checkbox_tick_uses_16px_shell_checkmark_asset` locks the image-pixels command and 16x16 size; the component atlas screenshot was refreshed under `docs/tests/editor`.

The 2026-06-30 radio dot micro-geometry pass keeps the radio mark sizing rule in `template_selection_control_geometry/metrics.rs` and the regression in `template_selection_controls_tests/marks.rs`. The default `RADIO_DOT_SIZE` is now 5 px for the 16 px mark, keeping the selected dot below 40% of the mark width while still honoring `TemplatePaneNodeData.value_number` for authored overrides. This follows the UE Slate/Starship pattern of small radio/check indicators inside a fixed 16 px control brush without adding a style-selector special case.

The 2026-06-30 toggle low-emphasis pass keeps toggle shape and tone split across the existing owners. `template_selection_control_geometry/metrics.rs` now sets the default toggle thumb to 12 px inside the 34 x 18 track, while `workbench_selection_control` selectors resolve checked border/thumb tones to the muted separator/text roles instead of the previous bright accent/white block. Projection and `.zui` declarations carry the same 12 px and `#414b54`/`#a4aeb4` values so the component atlas exercises the shared path rather than authored cyan overrides.

The 2026-07-01 selection-control enabled-label pass keeps checkbox/radio label hierarchy inside `style_selector/workbench_selection_control`. `palette.rs` now defines the fallback mark label token as `PALETTE.text_muted`, while `colors.rs::mark_label(...)` still returns `PALETTE.text_disabled` for disabled or loading states before checking declared label colors. The selector regression `selection_controls_enabled_labels_keep_readable_muted_tone` locks enabled checkbox/radio labels away from the disabled tone, and the refreshed component atlas under `docs/tests/editor` shows the Inputs And Selection labels as readable secondary text.

The 2026-06-30 search field glass slot pass keeps search-input glyph ownership in `template_fields/search.rs`. The 2026-06-30 dropdown chevron slot pass keeps field action-icon rendering in `template_dropdown_glyphs` instead of adding a dropdown special case to the root painter. `metrics.rs` defines the right-side action slot as 16 px, matching the Workbench Icon16 row affordance scale, and `chevron.rs` first renders the semantic `dropdown` shell SVG through `template_icon_assets::push_icon_asset_pixels(...)` tinted with the dropdown style chevron color. The former three-segment chevron remains only as an asset-missing fallback. `template_dropdowns/text.rs` continues to reserve the chevron slot for label layout, and `template_dropdowns_tests/paint.rs` locks the 16x16 image-pixels command so the component atlas cannot silently regress to hand-drawn fragments.

The 2026-07-06 dropdown chevron fallback glyph typed-grid follow-up keeps that asset-missing fallback explicit but removes the remaining raw float segment tuples from the chevron leaf. `template_dropdown_glyphs/segments.rs` now owns `DropdownGlyphSegmentSpec` plus the 14-unit chevron grid projection, while `chevron.rs` declares integer grid segments. The primary route still renders the shared `dropdown` shell SVG first; the typed grid only controls deterministic fallback pixels and continues to scale with `workbench_dropdown_metrics().chevron_size`.

The 2026-07-01 button label font-weight bridge keeps label style derivation inside `template_buttons/content`. `metrics.rs` maps `TemplatePaneNodeData.font_weight >= 600` to `UiTextRunPaintStyle { strong: true, .. }`; `entry.rs` uses that style for both runtime text measurement and the final label command; `text.rs` forwards the style into `HostPaintCommand::text(...)` instead of forcing every button label back to the default run style. The 2026-07-02 global typography preference pass supersedes that temporary mono decision for current Asset Browser utility tabs. The 2026-07-04 retained runtime text family preference passthrough keeps that same rule at the runtime export boundary. List/tree/property leaf owners consume these projections, and their text commands keep `UiTextRunPaintStyle::default()` rather than selecting a concrete family. This leaves font family selection with `HostTextPreferences` while still allowing global color theme and control-style preferences to flow through the row controls.

## Boundary Rules

- Keep `paint_template_nodes/mod.rs` limited to child declarations and re-exports.
- Keep draw iteration, clipping, and test image buffers in `template_node_pipeline/`; do not reintroduce wrapper functions in the root module.
- Keep per-node command construction in `template_nodes/` and low-level replay/test harness behavior in `render_commands.rs`.
- Keep Workbench pane, menu, dock, and overlay painters as consumers of `draw_template_nodes`/`has_template_nodes`; they should not reach into template-node internals.

## Validation Notes

The 2026-06-21 root re-export split reduced `paint_template_nodes/mod.rs` from 118 lines to an 85-line structural entry. The stable entries now re-export `draw_template_nodes`, `has_template_nodes`, `paint_template_nodes_for_test`, and `paint_template_nodes_for_test_with_background` directly from `template_node_pipeline`, while `paint_runtime_render_commands_for_test` remains re-exported from `render_commands`.

Validation used `cargo fmt -p zircon_editor`, `cargo fmt -p zircon_editor --check`, a paint-template-nodes root re-export ownership scan, scoped trailing-whitespace scan, and scoped `git diff --check`; package-level Cargo check and full Cargo tests remain deferred per the user's feature-first instruction.

The 2026-06-21 style-selector export split reduced `paint_template_nodes/style_selector/mod.rs` from 101 lines to a 20-line structural declaration/re-export entry. `style_selector/exports.rs` owns the restricted selector export surface for state projection and Workbench family selectors. Validation used `cargo fmt -p zircon_editor`, `cargo fmt -p zircon_editor --check`, a style-selector export ownership scan, scoped trailing-whitespace scan, and scoped `git diff --check`; package-level Cargo check and full Cargo tests remain deferred per the user's feature-first instruction.

The 2026-06-26 tab-like button pass keeps tab classification in `style_selector/workbench_button/tab_like.rs`, style selection in `workbench_button/selection.rs`, and the selected-state underline paint in `template_buttons/surface.rs`. `template_buttons/identity.rs` only routes recognized tab-like controls into the Workbench button path, including PageTab, DockTab, Asset Browser kind/view/tool controls authored without explicit variants, and the top Workbench module tabs. Module command buttons remain outside that tab-like whitelist so command styling can be tuned separately. This prevents cyan focus-ring rectangles from becoming a fallback behavior while keeping the root `paint_template_nodes` entries structural.

The 2026-06-26 Workbench module-tab clear-toolbar follow-up keeps top module tab styling inside the same Workbench button owners. `style_selector/workbench_button/tab_like.rs` exposes module-tab classification to sibling style code, and `workbench_button/selection.rs` gives inactive module tabs a transparent surface plus muted text while preserving low-emphasis hover/open/active feedback. Asset Browser tab-like controls keep their own low-contrast surface behavior, so this removes top-toolbar blocky tiles without flattening content tabs.

The 2026-06-26 Asset Browser filter/view tab follow-up keeps Content Browser-like filter and view toggles in the Workbench button style owner without treating every inactive item as a filled tile. `style_selector/workbench_button/tab_like.rs` exposes Asset Browser kind/view/utility-tab classification separately from PageTab, DockTab, and Workbench module tabs. `workbench_button/selection.rs` now gives inactive Asset Browser tab-like controls a transparent surface plus muted text, while hover, popup-open, focused, selected, and checked states still use the low-emphasis hover surface and the shared 2 px underline indicator from `template_buttons/surface.rs`. The regression coverage in `template_buttons_tests/style.rs` and `template_buttons_tests/paint.rs` locks transparent inactive tabs, hover feedback, and selected underline behavior.

The 2026-06-30 Asset Browser toolbar filter/view compound follow-up keeps the later de-emphasis in the same leaf owners. `workbench_button.rs` re-exports the Asset Browser tab-like classifier so the style selector export surface and button paint surface can share that identity without adding another root painter branch. `workbench_button/selection.rs` now routes Asset Browser tab-like selected/checked/open states to `PALETTE.surface_pressed` instead of the brighter hover surface, while `template_buttons/surface.rs` insets the selected underline only for Asset Browser tab-like controls. Page tabs, dock tabs, and top module tabs keep their existing full-width indicator geometry. `template_buttons_tests/style.rs` and `template_buttons_tests/paint.rs` lock the muted selected fill and inset underline so future toolbar composition work cannot reintroduce blocky filter/view tabs.

The 2026-06-26 toolbar icon glyph follow-up maps the Workbench top toolbar to the UE SlimToolbar `Icon20x20` sizing rule. `template_icon_buttons/geometry.rs` now gives Toolbar icon buttons a 20 px glyph cap/default for 30 px buttons while leaving Panel and Rail contexts on their existing density paths. The file fallback glyph owners for document, folder, and save icons draw recognizable document-plus, folder, and floppy outlines, so missing SVG assets no longer collapse into tiny placeholder line fragments.

The 2026-06-26 toolbar icon asset follow-up moves that fidelity step into the icon-button glyph owner without turning the root painter into an asset policy module. `template_icon_button_glyphs.rs` first asks `visual_assets/asset.rs` for an existing SVG/icon raster at the arranged glyph size, applies the current state foreground tint, and emits one image-pixel command when the asset exists. Missing icon paths deliberately return `None` from that existing-asset helper instead of generating the deterministic placeholder image, so the same glyph owner can fall back to the manual line-glyph vocabulary. This keeps UE-like toolbar icons asset-driven while preserving deterministic pixels for deferred or intentionally unmapped icon names.

The 2026-07-06 icon-button selector metrics follow-up keeps toolbar/panel/rail icon button radius and border width on the current host control metrics route. `style_selector/workbench_icon_button/selection/border.rs` now projects `icon_border_width(...)` from `current_host_metrics().border_width`, and `selection/radius.rs` projects rail/toolbar/panel fallback radius plus the panel declared-radius guard from `current_host_metrics().radius_control`. The selector exposes pure projection helpers only to its local tests. This slice intentionally leaves icon-button palette roles in the existing selector leaves for a later palette-only pass.

Validation for the icon-button selector metrics follow-up used scoped rustfmt, an icon-button selector scan returning `NO_ICON_BUTTON_SELECTOR_STATIC_METRIC_MATCHES`, and a target/external target editor screenshot scan returning clean. Focused Cargo and screenshot refresh remain deferred while cargo/rustc lanes are active and D target storage is tight, so this entry is not final toolbar icon button or window-level visual evidence.

The 2026-07-06 icon-button selector palette follow-up closes that remaining palette side without changing the icon-button state matrix. `style_selector/workbench_icon_button/palette.rs` now owns `WorkbenchIconButtonPalette` and projects normal, muted, panel surface/border, disabled, error, selected, pressed, hover, focus, text, accent, and shell-background roles from `current_host_palette()`. `selection/background.rs`, `border.rs`, `glyph.rs`, and the PrimaryImport branch in `selection.rs` consume that owner instead of reading static `PALETTE`; PrimaryImport also uses the current host border width instead of a fixed 1 px border. Declared background, border, and icon-color overrides keep their existing priority.

The 2026-07-06 Workbench button surface-indicator owner-route follow-up moves tab-like underline chrome out of `template_buttons/surface.rs` and into `template_buttons/surface_indicator.rs`. `WorkbenchButtonSurfaceIndicatorMetrics` projects underline height and Asset Browser tab inset from `HostControlMetrics`, while `WorkbenchButtonSurfaceIndicatorPalette` projects the underline accent from `HostMaterialPalette`. `button_surface_indicator_rect(...)` owns the bottom-edge rect and Asset Browser inset clamp, preserving the existing selected/checked-only underline behavior and avoiding a focused-only underline.

The 2026-07-06 Workbench button geometry metrics owner-route follow-up keeps button paint geometry on the same owner path. `template_buttons/metrics.rs` now owns `WorkbenchButtonGeometryMetrics`, projecting the default control radius and Add Component vertical offset from `HostControlMetrics`. `template_buttons/geometry.rs` consumes that projection while still honoring node-declared corner radius first, clamping to half height, and applying authored layout offsets after pixel alignment.

Validation for the geometry metrics owner route used scoped rustfmt, a geometry leaf scan returning `NO_BUTTON_GEOMETRY_LEAF_DIRECT_HOST_OR_OFFSET_RATIO_MATCHES`, small line-count checks, a repo target screenshot scan returning `NO_TARGET_EDITOR_WORKBENCH_BUTTON_GEOMETRY_SCREENSHOT_MATCHES`, and focused `cargo test -p zircon_editor --lib button_geometry_metrics_project_from_host_control_metrics --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-editor-button-surface-indicator-0706 --color never` passing 1/1 with existing warnings. `docs/tests/editor` screenshot refresh remains deferred, so this entry is not final Add Component, toolbar button, button group, or window-level visual evidence.

The 2026-07-06 Workbench button content metrics owner-route follow-up keeps label typography and content offsets in `template_buttons/content/metrics.rs`. `WorkbenchButtonContentMetrics` now projects label font size, line-height ratio, text clip guard, utility-tab padding, toolbar-chip padding, ordinary button padding, and pressed y offset from `HostControlMetrics`. Label layout consumers use that content metrics snapshot while measured ink width continues through `measure_runtime_text_width_with_style(...)`, keeping physical font selection on the runtime text preference path rather than a component-local font.

Validation for the content metrics owner route used scoped rustfmt, a direct-host-field scan returning `NO_BUTTON_CONTENT_METRICS_DIRECT_HOST_FIELD_READS`, small line-count checks, and a repo target screenshot scan returning `NO_TARGET_EDITOR_WORKBENCH_BUTTON_CONTENT_SCREENSHOT_MATCHES`. A focused Cargo follow-up for the 2026-07-06 Workbench button state/command/selection palette slices ran `cargo test -p zircon_editor --lib workbench_button --locked --jobs 1 --target-dir E:\cargo-targets\zircon-editor-button-palette-0706 --color never` against an external E-drive target directory. The slices therefore remain lightweight-evidence only until the focused tests and `docs/tests/editor` screenshots can be rerun successfully.

The 2026-06-26 document-tab close follow-up keeps the dock-tab close affordance in icon-button owners rather than template-specific paint code. `template_icon_buttons/style.rs` treats `DockTabClose`, `PageTabClose`, `DocumentTabClose`, and sibling tab-close ids as Toolbar context buttons, so normal state does not draw a panel surface. `template_icon_button_glyph_kind/mapping.rs` maps close/dismiss names to the `Close` glyph kind, and the chrome glyph dispatch draws the X-shaped close icon. In the same slice, `template_buttons/content/metrics.rs` measures labels with the node-declared font size before `template_buttons/content/text.rs` emits the text command; this keeps 12 px document-tab labels readable instead of under-measuring them with the 10 px body default.

The 2026-07-05 document-tab clip-guard centering pass keeps the latest text-spacing correction inside `template_buttons/content`. Button labels now expose measured ink width separately from the guarded text slot width: `template_buttons/content/entry.rs` centers icon+title by actual ink, while `label_text_slot_width(...)` adds `text_clip_guard` only to the emitted text frame. This prevents DockTab/PageTab labels such as `editor base.zui` from being visually pulled off center by a clipping reserve while retaining the guard needed by retained text rasterization. `document_tab_button_centers_icon_and_title_ink_without_clip_guard_bias` locks the current fallback document-tab path with `folder-open-outline`.

The 2026-06-26 command-button follow-up adds that separate command styling owner in `style_selector/workbench_button/command.rs`. It recognizes Compile and asset import command controls/actions and lets `workbench_button/selection.rs` replace authored accent-filled surfaces with muted Workbench surfaces plus accent foreground. Pixel regressions in `template_buttons` lock this as button style behavior, not root-module or screenshot-only logic.

The 2026-06-27 generic editor primary-button follow-up keeps the same button owner boundary but clamps legacy Material class colors before they can override Workbench tokens. `template_buttons/identity.rs` still routes generic editor variants such as `button_variant=primary` into the Workbench button path, but `style_selector/workbench_button/selection.rs` now applies declared background, border, and foreground colors only when `uses_workbench_visual_language(node)` is true. As a result, authored Workbench controls can still tune their local surface and border, while ordinary editor actions such as `OpenAssetsView` use the canonical low-emphasis primary surface instead of inheriting the old purple `.primary` rule from projection. The `editor_variant_button_ignores_legacy_declared_material_colors` regression captures that boundary.

The 2026-06-30 Primary/Danger button follow-up narrows that declared-color boundary further for high-signal commands. `style_selector/workbench_button/palette.rs` now defines Primary and Danger chrome on the neutral Workbench command surface/border ladder, while `selection.rs` only lets declared background and border colors affect Workbench button kinds other than Primary and Danger. Primary also ignores declared foreground so it stays on the canonical low-emphasis command text. Danger keeps semantic foreground only, allowing delete text and glyphs to remain error-colored without repainting the entire button as a red slab. `states/interactive.rs` keeps focused buttons on the shared `focus_ring`; `template_buttons_tests/style.rs` and `template_buttons_tests/paint.rs` lock the resolved style and pixel output so the root painter stays a consumer of button style rather than a special-case policy owner.

The 2026-07-02 Asset Browser toolbar-chip follow-up keeps kind/view filter controls in the existing button owners while splitting their visual semantics from true tabs. `style_selector/workbench_button/tab_like.rs` classifies Asset Browser filter/view controls as toolbar chips and utility buttons as true tabs; `style_selector/workbench_button/selection.rs` gives selected chips a low-emphasis segmented surface plus 1 px neutral border; `template_buttons/surface.rs` skips the tab underline only for toolbar chips; and `template_buttons/content/metrics.rs` keeps the toolbar-chip label slot compact without assigning a font family. This keeps `All/Texture/Material/List/Thumb` from looking like selected tabs while preserving the tab indicator on Preview/References/Metadata/Plugins. Text still flows through `UiTextRunPaintStyle` and the retained text preference chain, so the actual font family remains globally switchable through typography preferences.

The 2026-07-02 toolbar-chip pass validates this owner split with focused `asset_browser_toolbar_chip` 4/4, focused `asset_browser_utility_tab` 4/4, hover/density focused coverage 2/2, direct M3 screenshot harness 1/1, and an editor-host build in `D:\cargo-targets\zircon-editor-thumbnail-visual-0702`. Screenshot evidence is under `docs/tests/editor`: `editor-window-m3-asset-browser-900x620.png` modified `2026-07-02 06:26:37 +08:00`, 71763 bytes, SHA256 `2C3A1BF50B8BAB94A10A1CC02B0C59C338373BCEF5488A852DC45C5BFF87490A`; `asset-browser-toolbar-chip-crop-20260702.png` modified `2026-07-02 06:27:47 +08:00`, 4046 bytes, SHA256 `446C57204396E99C7CDCA1D1B413D4B5AC4BA7C00735683378FC51C5D9A4744F`; `asset-browser-toolbar-chip-crop-20260702-3x.png` modified `2026-07-02 06:27:47 +08:00`, 8220 bytes, SHA256 `FE86997639FA54F8A545F7E7EACEB4EF01CBA28F6E3870A5148087AD4F4C573C`. Repo `target` same-name screenshot scan returned 0.

The 2026-06-26 table selected-row follow-up keeps Workbench table row selection split between style and paint owners. `style_selector/workbench_table_row` owns the selected fill decision, rejects authored selected backgrounds for marked rows, and now suppresses the focus-ring border for rows that are selected/checked while still letting unmarked focused rows show the keyboard focus border. `template_table_rows/surface.rs` paints only the 2 px accent selection indicator. The row still uses the normal table text/action command path, so the selected state is visible without turning the full row into a cyan block or cyan outline.

The later 2026-06-29 row selected-outline pass updates this table-row contract from a left indicator to a full row outline. Marked table rows use the shared selected surface and accent border, and the paint owner suppresses its separator for selected rows so the bottom outline is not overwritten. The table text and action command paths still stay unchanged.

The 2026-06-30 row/popup selected-fill de-emphasis pass keeps that outline but changes the fill contract from the shared selected surface to `PALETTE.surface_pressed` for table, list, tree, and popup row families. This keeps selection visible through the accent outline without turning row interiors into cyan blocks.

The 2026-06-26 table recessed-surface follow-up keeps Workbench table list chrome in the same table-row owner. `style_selector/workbench_table_row/palette.rs` now maps header and tail/empty-fill backgrounds to the same recessed row surface used by ordinary rows, matching the Unreal Slate `TableView` pattern and removing extra black bands around Asset Browser tables. `style_selector/workbench_table_row/tests.rs` locks the behavior before wider table composition changes.

The 2026-06-27 table row action follow-up keeps inline row affordance visibility local to the table-row action leaf owner. `style_selector/workbench_table_row/state.rs` remains the single source for hot row states, and `template_table_rows/actions/entry.rs` now paints data-row `more-horizontal.svg` only for selected, checked, pressed, or hot rows. Table headers still paint their settings gear unconditionally. The regression in `template_table_rows_tests/paint.rs` locks neutral rows to zero action image-pixel commands while hovered and selected rows keep the shared shell SVG affordance.

The 2026-07-06 table action slot owner-route follow-up keeps that same affordance behavior but moves slot geometry and chrome behind `template_table_rows/actions/{metrics,palette}.rs`. `actions/metrics.rs` projects the action column width, 20 px default button slot, 16 px default icon rect, border width, and radius from `HostControlMetrics`; both `actions/geometry.rs` and `cells/geometry.rs` consume the same action column width so cell allocation and the right-side action slot cannot drift. `actions/palette.rs` projects data-row hover slot, header pressed slot, and border colors from `HostMaterialPalette`, while `actions/entry.rs` remains only the paint consumer.

The 2026-07-06 table action fallback glyph scalable-grid follow-up keeps hand-drawn gear/kebab fallback geometry responsive to the action icon rect. `template_table_rows/actions/glyphs/segments.rs` owns `GlyphSegmentSpec` and the 16-unit projection used by both `gear.rs` and `kebab.rs`; those shape files now declare integer grid segments instead of fixed pixel `FrameRect`s. The normal SVG asset paths remain the primary route, and the fallback output stays unchanged at the default 16 px action icon size while scaling with future `WorkbenchTableActionMetrics::icon_size` changes.

The 2026-07-06 table cell metrics/text owner-route follow-up keeps table cell typography, padding, and text clip guard behind `template_table_rows/cells/metrics.rs`. `cells/commands.rs` consumes the projected body font size, line height, and clip guard while keeping right-aligned text width on `measure_runtime_text_width`, so retained table text still follows the runtime text measurement route. `cells/geometry.rs` consumes the same projected horizontal and vertical insets for cell allocation; column ratios and drop order remain table-local layout rules rather than host appearance preferences.

The 2026-07-06 table column readable metrics/runtime text follow-up keeps table column readability thresholds behind `template_table_rows/cells/metrics.rs`. `WorkbenchTableColumnMetrics` projects minimum widths from host row height, gaps, text clip guard, and `measure_runtime_text_width` samples. `cells/allocation.rs` consumes one metrics snapshot for ratios, minimum widths, and drop order so column drop, clamp, and overflow recovery use the same values, while `cells/commands.rs` consumes `TABLE_COLUMN_COUNT` instead of the ratio array. The old fixed `TABLE_COLUMN_MIN_WIDTHS` and `[120.0, 56.0, 56.0, 72.0]` production route is removed.

The 2026-07-06 table row surface metrics owner-route follow-up keeps row chrome size details behind `template_table_rows/metrics.rs`. `WorkbenchTableRowSurfaceMetrics` projects the row radius from `radius_control - border_width`, preserving the current 3 px default while still following global radius and border preference changes, and projects separator height from the host border width. `surface.rs` remains the paint-command consumer for row background, border, selected-row separator suppression, and tail/selected paint rect offsets; it no longer owns table-specific radius or separator literals.

The 2026-06-30 tree row action icon-button slot follow-up applies the same low-emphasis action-slot language to Workbench tree rows. `template_row_metrics.rs` now defines tree action icons at 16 px plus a 20 px button slot, `template_tree_row_geometry/actions.rs` owns the relative slot and centered icon rects, and `template_tree_rows/actions.rs` draws the neutral slot before the eye, lock, or more-vertical shared shell SVG/fallback glyph. The command-level regression in `template_tree_rows_tests/paint.rs` locks selected eye/more and normal eye/lock actions to 20 px slots, 16 px icons, neutral border/radius, icon containment, and slot-before-icon z order. Validation used scoped rustfmt, the focused action-slot test, `template_tree_rows` 10/10, component-atlas and M3 screenshot harnesses, editor-host build, manual atlas review, and target screenshot scans.

The 2026-07-06 tree row fallback glyph scalable-grid follow-up keeps hand-drawn tree glyphs responsive to their actual rects. `template_tree_row_glyphs/segments.rs` now owns `GlyphSegmentSpec` and three grid baselines for action, disclosure, and object glyphs, matching the existing 16 px, 12 px, and 14 px default rects. Disclosure chevrons, action glyphs, and object fallback glyphs declare integer grid segments instead of fixed `local_rect(...)` pixel coordinates, so the default visual output stays stable while future row-density changes can scale fallback glyphs with the component metrics.

The 2026-07-06 tree indent guide metrics/geometry follow-up keeps guide-line geometry out of the tree surface paint leaf. `template_row_metrics.rs` now carries guide width, vertical extension, and opacity with the rest of the row density values; `template_tree_row_geometry/rows.rs` owns `tree_guide_rect(...)`; and `template_tree_rows/surface.rs` consumes the guide color, rect, and opacity instead of spelling out fixed 1 px width, 2 px total extension, or `0.78` opacity. The default drawing remains visually unchanged while row density and border width can move the guide line through the shared metrics route.

The 2026-07-06 list-row adornment follow-up keeps the right-side check/chevron/disabled glyph lane in the shared row metrics and palette owners. `template_row_metrics.rs` now projects `list_adornment_size` and `list_adornment_right_inset` from `current_host_metrics()`, preserving the current 16 px / 12 px Slate-like default while letting density and typography changes move list rows with tree and property rows. `template_list_row_glyphs/geometry.rs` consumes those row metrics, and the disabled diamond fallback now receives `workbench_row_palette().disabled_adornment_tint` from its caller instead of reading static `PALETTE.text_disabled`; the normal SVG tint and fallback tint therefore stay on the same appearance route.

The 2026-07-06 list-row fallback glyph scalable-grid follow-up keeps the hand-drawn fallback geometry responsive to the adornment rect. `template_list_row_glyphs/segments.rs` now owns `GlyphSegmentSpec` and the 16-unit grid projection, while the check, chevron, and disabled-diamond shape files declare integer grid segments instead of fixed pixel `FrameRect`s. The default 16 px output is preserved, but fallback glyphs now scale with `list_adornment_size` when the shared row metrics change.

Validation for the list-row adornment follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a glyph-owner scan returning `NO_LIST_ROW_GLYPH_STATIC_PALETTE_OR_OLD_METRIC_MATCHES`, and a production-row-metrics scan returning `NO_ROW_METRICS_PRODUCTION_STATIC_LIST_ADORNMENT_MATCHES`. A private disabled-diamond fallback regression locks the supplied tint path. Focused Cargo and screenshot refresh remain deferred while other cargo/rustc lanes are active, so this entry is not final list/selection-list screenshot evidence.

The 2026-07-06 list-row layer-order follow-up keeps row command stacking in `template_list_rows/layers.rs`. That owner now names the selected-indicator, label, and trailing-adornment orders, while `template_list_rows.rs` and `template_list_rows/surface.rs` only consume helpers when dispatching surface, text, and adornment commands. This preserves the current selected-indicator behavior, but prevents list-row leaves from accumulating anonymous `order + N` offsets as selection lists grow into richer row content.

Validation for the list-row layer-order follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a static scan returning `NO_TEMPLATE_LIST_ROWS_DIRECT_ORDER_OFFSETS`, and a repo `target` list/row/layer/editor/workbench screenshot scan with no matches. Focused Cargo and `docs/tests/editor` screenshot refresh remain deferred, so this entry is not final complex-list, selection-list, or window-level visual evidence.

The 2026-07-06 table-row layer-order follow-up keeps table row stacking in `template_table_rows/layers.rs`. That owner now names separator, cell text, action slot, and action icon orders, while `template_table_rows/commands.rs`, `template_table_rows/surface.rs`, and `template_table_rows/actions/entry.rs` only consume helpers when dispatching surface, cell text, action slot, SVG asset, and fallback glyph commands. This preserves current Asset Browser table behavior, but prevents table row leaves from accumulating anonymous `order + N` offsets as table/list rows gain richer action content.

Validation for the table-row layer-order follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a static scan returning `NO_TEMPLATE_TABLE_ROWS_DIRECT_ORDER_OFFSETS`, and a repo `target` table/row/layer/editor/workbench screenshot scan with no matches. This preserves current Scene Outliner-style row behavior, but prevents tree row leaves from accumulating anonymous `order + N` offsets as tree rows gain richer disclosure and action content.

The 2026-07-06 property-row layer-order follow-up keeps Inspector property row stacking in `template_property_rows/layers.rs`. The 2026-07-06 Transform axis-label text-owner follow-up hard-cut the flat `template_axis_labels/text.rs` leaf into `template_axis_labels/text/{mod,geometry,style}.rs`. `text/geometry.rs` now owns the axis-label text rectangle, including vertical centering and the minimum extent clamp. `text/style.rs` packages the resolved axis-label color, host-projected font size and line height, and the runtime-facing `UiTextRunPaintStyle` route. `text/mod.rs` keeps only text fallback/trim logic, metrics lookup, layer-order passthrough, and final `HostPaintCommand::text` emission.

The 2026-07-06 Transform axis-label Scale-link geometry/style follow-up keeps the link glyph split like the text path. `template_axis_labels/scale_link/geometry.rs` owns the double-lobe and connector rectangles plus the origin calculation. `template_axis_labels/scale_link/style.rs` owns the lobe outline style and the filled connector style, including the connector's no-border, no-radius command policy. `template_axis_labels/scale_link.rs` now reads the metrics snapshot, asks the geometry and style owners for their data, applies the named connector layer order, and emits the final quad commands.

The 2026-07-06 Transform axis-label color style owner split follow-up keeps ordinary axis-label color semantics separate from Scale-link color semantics. The old flat `template_axis_labels/style.rs` file is replaced by `template_axis_labels/style/{mod,axis,scale_link}.rs`: `axis.rs` owns disabled axis colors, declared label-color alpha gating, and the Transform Scale-axis prefix fallback, while `scale_link.rs` owns Scale-link normal and disabled palette projection. `style/mod.rs` only re-exports those private owner functions back to the axis-label paint subtree.

The 2026-07-06 Transform axis-label identity role/control-id owner split follow-up keeps node identity routing out of one flat helper file. The old `template_axis_labels/identity.rs` file is replaced by `template_axis_labels/identity/{mod,control_ids,roles}.rs`: `roles.rs` owns the Label/Icon/SvgIcon allowlist, `control_ids.rs` owns Transform axis suffix matching, the Scale-link control id, and the Scale-axis prefix helper, and `mod.rs` owns the narrow `AxisLabelKind` boundary plus node-level orchestration. `style/axis.rs` now consumes the identity helper for Scale-axis detection instead of carrying a sibling copy of the control-id prefix rule.

The 2026-07-06 Transform axis-label metrics model/projection owner split follow-up keeps metrics declaration and host projection in separate files. The old `template_axis_labels/metrics.rs` file is replaced by `template_axis_labels/metrics/{mod,model,projection}.rs`: `model.rs` only declares `AxisLabelMetrics`, `projection.rs` owns the `current_host_metrics()` projection plus named Scale-link lobe width, overlap, radius, and minimum extent factors, and `mod.rs` only re-exports the private owner surface. This keeps the prior default 11 px label font, 13.2 px line height, 6x7 link lobe, 2 px overlap, and 1 px connector behavior while removing anonymous projection factors from the flat owner.

Validation for the axis-label metrics owner split used scoped rustfmt, flat-file and owner-file scans returning `NO_AXIS_LABEL_METRICS_FLAT_FILE` and `AXIS_LABEL_METRICS_OWNER_FILES_PRESENT`, a `mod.rs` structure scan returning `NO_AXIS_LABEL_METRICS_MOD_PROJECTION_LOGIC`, a projection scan returning `NO_AXIS_LABEL_METRICS_PROJECTION_RAW_FACTORS`, and a repo `target` axis/label/metrics/projection/transform/editor/workbench screenshot scan with no matches. The 2026-07-06 Transform controls component visual screenshot route adds a dedicated `retained_menu_pointer/transform_visual_screenshot.rs` test module rather than expanding the large workbench atlas file. The route samples Location, Rotation, and Scale rows through the real retained painters for X/Y/Z axis labels, compact numeric value fields, focused and disabled field states, and the Scale-link glyph. Its ignored artifact test outputs `editor-components-transform-controls-900x360.png` under `docs/tests/editor`, and the focused non-ignored test asserts visible pixels for representative axis-label, field, and Scale-link regions. This is component-level evidence only; it is not final Inspector Transform or full-window visual completion.

The 2026-07-07 Property/axis component visual screenshot route adds a dedicated `retained_menu_pointer/property_axis_visual_screenshot.rs` module for the next Inspector slice. The route composes real property-row, axis-label, axis-value-field, and Scale-link retained painters into Material/Roughness/Location rows, Position/Rotation/Scale groups, and focused/pressed/disabled state samples. The 2026-07-08 Property/axis field-state closeout extends that local route with selected-only and focused scalar value rows plus selected, focused, and disabled Transform numeric fields. The 2026-07-06 ordinary button component visual screenshot route adds a dedicated `retained_menu_pointer/button_visual_screenshot.rs` module for the atomic button slice. The route uses the real retained button painters to sample filled and outlined text buttons, the Add Component semantic Icon16 path, dropdown chevron layout, UE/Slate-style pressed content offset, disabled muted labels, and tab-like selected/hovered underline behavior. The route uses the real retained field painters to sample an ordinary input value, an import-path placeholder, the shared search icon plus placeholder inset, a focused search query, Workbench stepper dividers/arrows, a focused neutral border, and disabled muted fallback text. The 2026-06-26 status-bar flat-control follow-up keeps status chip/icon behavior inside the status-control owners. `style_selector/workbench_status_control` makes normal chip/icon surfaces transparent while retaining interaction feedback, and `template_status_controls` skips transparent quads instead of painting button-like blocks. Status chips no longer draw the down-chevron; text spacing now uses `METRICS.gap_s`, while `template_status_controls/chips/text.rs` continues to own label/value splitting and right alignment.

The 2026-07-04 status-chip value-layer follow-up keeps the right-side `Grid`/`Snap`/`Zoom` hierarchy in those same owners. `style_selector/workbench_status_control/model.rs` now carries separate `label_text` and `value_text` colors, and `template_status_controls/chips/text.rs` paints colon labels with the inherited muted/right-label color while values use the normal primary text color. `workbench_window_projection.rs` projects `status_right_label_color` only into status chip labels, so `workbench_status_bar.zui` can keep UE-like muted labels without forcing `10 cm`, `On`, or `100%` into the same low-emphasis color.

The 2026-06-26 status-signal marker follow-up keeps bottom-left status signals as one inline `METRICS.gap_m` round marker plus one text run. Signal geometry ignores legacy per-asset `icon_size`, `layout_icon_size`, vertical icon offsets, and stroke/mark widths; `template_status_glyphs/signals.rs` now paints the same circle for Ready, Success, Warning, and Info. The old success/check, warning-triangle, and info-circle signal glyph modules were removed so the status bar cannot drift back to large composite icons through a compatibility path.

The 2026-06-26 status-bar spacing follow-up keeps horizontal density authored in `workbench_status_bar.zui` without adding another paint owner. The status bar root gap is zero, left signal and right chip/icon slots have compact fixed widths, and the stretch filler is a transparent `Space` with no control id. Bridge tests assert the filler and idle task slot do not project host contract nodes, so the painter cannot accidentally render the middle stretch as an empty bordered control.

The 2026-07-04 material feedback progress follow-up keeps progress/backdrop styling inside `template_material_feedback` instead of adding a root painter branch or static local palette. `template_material_feedback/palette.rs` projects track, workbench fill, disabled roles, tone roles, and backdrop scrim from `current_host_palette()`, while `template_material_feedback/metrics.rs` projects the linear radius floor and circular indeterminate percent from `current_host_metrics()`. Linear and circular progress, tone color resolution, and backdrop fallback consume those two owners, so later appearance/theme changes can move progress bars with the rest of the retained-host controls. The component atlas now includes a `WorkbenchProgressAtlas` sample under Complex Content to keep this route visible in screenshot review.

The 2026-07-04 tooltip follow-up keeps popup help bubbles in the same host preference route. `template_tooltips/metrics.rs` projects bubble size, radius, border, shadow offset, text offsets, line heights, arrow size, and icon size from `current_host_metrics()`, while `style_selector/workbench_tooltip/palette.rs` projects normal, disabled, focused, and hover tooltip colors from `current_host_palette()`. The tooltip surface/text/glyph leaves only consume those owners plus declared node overrides, so component-local `TOOLTIP_*` dimensions and static production `PALETTE` reads cannot drift away from the rest of the Workbench. The component atlas now includes `WorkbenchTooltipAtlas` as an overlay sample in the popup/picker area.

The 2026-07-06 Workbench tooltip state palette-owner follow-up keeps that boundary inside the state selector as well. `style_selector/workbench_tooltip/palette.rs` now exposes `tooltip_normal_style_from_palette(...)`, and `style_selector/workbench_tooltip/state.rs` owns `tooltip_state_style_from_palette(...)` so disabled/loading, pressed, focused, hovered, and normal tooltip states consume one `WorkbenchTooltipPalette` projection instead of re-entering the global palette route per branch. Focused-only tooltips keep neutral bubble chrome, pressed tooltips keep the active border/icon, hovered tooltips keep the accent icon, and unavailable tooltips keep disabled surface/text/shadow roles.

The 2026-07-06 tooltip layer-order owner follow-up moves retained Workbench Tooltip internal paint order into `template_tooltips/layers.rs`. Tooltip commands consume named helpers for bubble, text, arrow, and info icon layers; the surface leaf consumes the bubble helper after drawing the shadow, and the text entry consumes `body_order(...)` after the title layer. This keeps tooltip shadow, bubble, text, arrow, and icon stacking local to the tooltip family before richer popovers or overlay help surfaces compose it.

Validation for the tooltip layer-order owner follow-up used scoped rustfmt, a static scan returning `NO_TEMPLATE_TOOLTIPS_DIRECT_ORDER_OFFSETS`, and a repo `target` tooltip/layer/editor/workbench screenshot scan with no matches. Focused Cargo and `docs/tests/editor` screenshot refresh remain deferred while other cargo/rustc lanes are active, so this entry is not final tooltip, popover, feedback-overlay, or window-level visual evidence.

The 2026-07-06 Workbench alert state palette-owner follow-up keeps alert tone and state roles behind the alert selector palette owner. `style_selector/workbench_alert/palette.rs` now owns `WorkbenchAlertPalette`, projected by `workbench_alert_palette_from_host(...)`, for info/success/warning/error tone surfaces, disabled/loading roles, and the pressed active border. `style_selector/workbench_alert/state.rs` consumes that owner instead of reading `current_host_palette()` or `HostMaterialPalette` directly, while keeping loading unavailable, focused-only tone border, and pressed active-border semantics unchanged. This follows the earlier alert static-palette route and prevents state leaves from growing their own host-palette projection logic as feedback components split into smaller owners.

The 2026-07-06 Workbench alert fallback glyph typed-grid follow-up keeps alert mark geometry on the same deterministic segment route as buttons, chips, dropdowns, field steppers, section titles, and toolbar icon buttons. `template_alert_glyphs/segments.rs` keeps exporting `ALERT_ICON_SIZE` for the existing inline alert layout, but the internal mark projection now uses `AlertGlyphSegmentSpec` and `alert_segment(...)` over the 18-unit alert icon grid. Close, info, success, and warning mark segments no longer pass raw float tuples; round surfaces and the warning triangle fill remain in their existing owners.

The 2026-07-06 icon button content style owner follow-up moves glyph color and resolved painter state projection into `template_icon_buttons/content/style.rs`. `IconButtonContentStyle` packages the resolved glyph tint and state from `WorkbenchIconButtonStyle`, while `template_icon_buttons/commands.rs` keeps identity gating, paint rect lookup, context lookup, glyph rect lookup, surface dispatch, and final glyph command dispatch. The 2026-07-06 icon button layer-order owner follow-up moves the glyph paint order into `template_icon_buttons/layers.rs`. The 2026-07-06 icon button UE pressed-offset feedback follow-up removes the short-lived glyph pressed overlay path and aligns icon buttons with the UE/Slate pressed-padding behavior recorded in the plan. `template_icon_buttons/content/style.rs` now maps `UiPainterResolvedState::Pressed` to `current_host_metrics().button_pressed_offset_y`, and `template_icon_buttons/commands.rs` applies that offset to the icon glyph rect before dispatching the glyph command. `template_icon_button_glyphs.rs` no longer accepts painter state or emits a pressed overlay command; `template_icon_buttons_tests/commands.rs` locks the command count and 1 px glyph y-offset. Validation used scoped rustfmt, static scans confirming the overlay file is gone and the glyph leaf has no pressed overlay/state references, a test-guard scan, and a repo `target` icon/button/pressed/editor/workbench screenshot scan with no matches; focused Cargo and `docs/tests/editor` screenshot refresh remain deferred while other cargo/rustc lanes are active.

The 2026-07-06 button surface command style owner follow-up gives normal Workbench buttons the same surface-command boundary as icon buttons. `template_buttons/surface/style.rs` now packages the resolved button fill, border, border width, and radius into `ButtonSurfaceCommandStyle`, including the existing `button_style(...)` and `button_radius(...)` lookups. `template_buttons/surface.rs` keeps final surface quad emission and the existing tab-like underline indicator dispatch. The 2026-07-06 button content style owner follow-up gives normal Workbench buttons a matching content-style boundary. `template_buttons/content/style.rs` now packages the resolved glyph color, text color, and pressed-state content y offset into `ButtonContentStyle`, including the existing `button_style(...)` and `content_offset_y(...)` lookups. `template_buttons/content/entry.rs` keeps label resolution, runtime text measurement, icon/chevron layout, asset fallback dispatch, and final glyph/text commands. The 2026-07-06 button content layout owner follow-up moves ordinary button content geometry into `template_buttons/content/layout.rs`. `ButtonContentLayout` now owns max-text-width, text-slot-width, content-width, minimum content extent, and the shared centered-offset helper, while `template_buttons/content/entry.rs` only consumes the layout snapshot after runtime text measurement. `template_buttons/content/glyph.rs` and `text.rs` consume the same centered-y helper, keeping icon and label vertical alignment on one route. The 2026-07-06 ordinary button UE Icon16 metrics follow-up raises the semantic button glyph/image slot from the previous 14 px default to the UE/Slate Icon16 baseline through host metrics. `template_button_glyphs/metrics.rs` now projects `font_large + border_width * 2` with a minimum guard, keeping small button glyph sizing on the same global density/font route as other retained-host controls. The 2026-07-06 section-title follow-up keeps Workbench section headings on the same host appearance route as buttons, chips, rows, dialogs, and command palette. `template_section_titles/style.rs` now owns `WorkbenchSectionTitlePalette` and `WorkbenchSectionTitleMetrics`, projecting title text roles, Mesh title tone, disabled text, font size, line height, left inset, and strong-text offset from `current_host_palette()` and `current_host_metrics()`. `template_section_title_glyphs/style.rs` and `metrics.rs` now own the matching glyph palette/metrics so ordinary heading icons use the host muted text role, Transform headings use the host disabled text role, and icon size/gap follow host typography and spacing.
